Singh</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label" style="text-align:left"><span class="nowrap">Succeeded by</span></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Chandra_Shekhar" title="Chandra Shekhar">Chandra Shekhar</a></td><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1066479718"></tr><tr><th colspan="2" class="infobox-header" style="background:lavender;line-height:normal;padding:0.2em;">22th <a href="/wiki/Leader_of_the_House_(Rajya_Sabha)" class="mw-redirect" title="Leader of the House (Rajya Sabha)">Leader of the House, Rajya Sabha</a></th></tr><tr><td colspan="2" class="infobox-full-data" style="border-bottom:none"><span class="nowrap"><b>In office</b></span><br />June 1996&#160;–&#32;November 1996</td><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1066479718"></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label" style="text-align:left"><span class="nowrap">Preceded by</span></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Sikander_Bakht" title="Sikander Bakht">Sikander Bakht</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label" style="text-align:left"><span class="nowrap">Succeeded by</span></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/H._D._Deve_Gowda" title="H. </td></tr><tr><th colspan="2" class="infobox-header" style="background:lavender">Personal details</th></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Born</th><td class="infobox-data"><span style="display:none">(<span class="bday">1919-12-04</span>)</span>4 December 1919<sup id="cite_ref-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-1">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup><br /><a href="/wiki/Sohawa_Tehsil" title="Sohawa Tehsil">Pari Darveza</a>, <a href="/wiki/Punjab_Province_(British_India)" title="Punjab Province (British India)">Punjab</a>, <a href="/wiki/British_Raj" title="British Raj">British India</a> <br /> <span style="font-size:85%;">(present-day <a href="/wiki/Punjab,_Pakistan" title="Punjab, Pakistan">Punjab</a>, Pakistan)</span></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Died</th><td class="infobox-data">30 November 2012<span style="display:none">(2012-11-30)</span> (aged&#160;92)<br /><a href="/wiki/Gurugram" class="mw-redirect" title="Gurugram">Gurugram</a>, <a href="/wiki/Haryana" title="Haryana">Haryana</a>, India
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1066479718"></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Monuments</th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Raj_Ghat_and_associated_memorials" title="Raj Ghat and associated memorials">Samata Sthal</a></td></tr><tr style="display:none"><td colspan="2">
</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Political party</th><td class="infobox-data"><div><ul><li><a href="/wiki/Indian_National_Congress" title="Indian National Congress">Indian National Congress</a> (1964–1988)</li><li><a href="/wiki/Janata_Dal" title="Janata Dal">Janata Dal</a> (1988–1998)</li><li><a href="/wiki/Bharatiya_Janata_Party" title="Bharatiya Janata Party">Bharatiya Janata Party</a> (1998–2012)</li></ul></div></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Spouse</th><td class="infobox-data"><style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1151524712">.mw-parser-output .marriage-line-margin2px{line-height:0;margin-bottom:-2px}.mw-parser-output .marriage-line-margin3px{line-height:0;margin-bottom:-3px}.mw-parser-output .marriage-display-ws{display:inline;white-space:nowrap}</style>
<div class="marriage-display-ws"><div style="display:inline-block;line-height:normal;margin-top:1px;white-space:normal;"><a href="/wiki/Sheila_Gujral" title="Sheila Gujral">Sheila Gujral</a></div>
   <div class="marriage-line-margin2px">&#8203;</div>&#32;<div style="display:inline-block;margin-bottom:1px;">&#8203;</div>&#40;<abbr title="married">m.</abbr>&#160;1945&#59;&#32;died&#160;2011&#41;<wbr />&#8203;</div></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Children</th><td class="infobox-data">2, including <a href="/wiki/Naresh_Gujral" title="Naresh Gujral">Naresh Gujral</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Parent</th><td class="infobox-data"><style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1126788409">.mw-parser-output .plainlist ol,.mw-parser-output .plainlist ul{line-height:inherit;list-style:none;margin:0;padding:0}.mw-parser-output .plainlist ol li,.mw-parser-output .plainlist ul li{margin-bottom:0}</style><div class="plainlist"><ul><li><a href="/wiki/Avtar_Narain_Gujral" title="Avtar Narain Gujral">Avtar Narain Gujral</a> (father)</li></ul></div></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Relatives</th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Satish_Gujral" title="Satish Gujral">Satish Gujral</a> (brother)</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Alma_mater" title="Alma mater">Alma mater</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Dayanand_Anglo-Vedic_Schools_System" class="mw-redirect" title="Dayanand Anglo-Vedic Schools System">D.A.V. College</a>,<br /><a href="/wiki/Hailey_College_of_Commerce" title="Hailey College of Commerce">Hailey College of Commerce</a><br /><a href="/wiki/Forman_Christian_College_University" class="mw-redirect" title="Forman Christian College University">Forman Christian College University</a><br /><a href="/wiki/Panjab_University" title="Panjab University">Panjab University</a></td></tr><tr><td colspan="2" class="infobox-below" style="border-top: 1px solid right; font-size: 95%"><div></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p><b>Inder Kumar Gujral</b> (4 December 1919 – 30 November 2012) was an Indian diplomat, politician and freedom activist who served as the <a href="/wiki/List_of_prime_ministers_of_India" title="List of prime ministers of India">12th prime minister of India</a> from April 1997 to March 1998.<sup id="cite_ref-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Born in <a href="/wiki/Punjab" title="Punjab">Punjab</a>, he was influenced by nationalistic ideas as a student, and joined the <a href="/wiki/All_India_Students_Federation" class="mw-redirect" title="All India Students Federation">All India Students Federation</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Communist_Party_of_India" title="Communist Party of India">Communist Party of India</a>. He was imprisoned for taking part in the <a href="/wiki/Quit_India_Movement" title="Quit India Movement">Quit India movement</a>. After independence, he joined the <a href="/wiki/Indian_National_Congress" title="Indian National Congress">Indian National Congress</a> party in 1964, and became a <a href="/wiki/Member_of_parliament_(India)" class="mw-redirect" title="Member of parliament (India)">Member of Parliament</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Rajya_Sabha" title="Rajya Sabha">Rajya Sabha</a>.
</p><p>He was the <a href="/wiki/Minister_of_Information_and_Broadcasting" class="mw-redirect" title="Minister of Information and Broadcasting">Minister of Information and Broadcasting</a> during the <a href="/wiki/The_Emergency_(India)" title="The Emergency (India)">emergency</a>. In 1976, he was appointed the <a href="/wiki/List_of_ambassadors_of_India_to_Russia" title="List of ambassadors of India to Russia">Ambassador of India to the Soviet Union</a>. In 1996, he became the <a href="/wiki/Minister_of_External_Affairs_(India)" title="Minister of External Affairs (India)">Minister of External Affairs</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Deve_Gowda_ministry" title="Deve Gowda ministry">Deve Gowda ministry</a>, and developed the <a href="/wiki/Gujral_doctrine" class="mw-redirect" title="Gujral doctrine">Gujral doctrine</a> during this period. He was appointed  the 12th <a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_India" title="Prime Minister of India">Prime Minister of India</a> in 1997. His tenure lasted for less than a year.
</p><p>He retired from all political positions in 1998. He died in 2012 at the age of 92, following hospitalization due to a <a href="/wiki/Lower_respiratory_tract_infection" title="Lower respiratory tract infection">lung infection</a>.
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Early_and_personal_life">Early and personal life</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Inder_Kumar_Gujral&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Early and personal life">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Family">Family</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Inder_Kumar_Gujral&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Family">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Inder Kumar Gujral was born on 4 December 1919 in a <a href="/wiki/Punjabi_Hindu" class="mw-redirect" title="Punjabi Hindu">Punjabi Hindu</a> <a href="/wiki/Khatri" title="Khatri">Khatri</a> family<sup id="cite_ref-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-4">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup> to <a href="/wiki/Avtar_Narain_Gujral" title="Avtar Narain Gujral">Avtar Narain</a> and Pushpa Gujral in the village of Pari Darveza in the <a href="/wiki/Sohawa_Tehsil" title="Sohawa Tehsil">Sohawa Tehsil</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Jhelum_District" title="Jhelum District">Jhelum District</a> in <a href="/wiki/Punjab_Province_(British_India)" title="Punjab Province (British India)">undivided Punjab</a> in <a href="/wiki/British_Raj" title="British Raj">British India</a>, which is in present-day <a href="/wiki/Punjab,_Pakistan" title="Punjab, Pakistan">Punjab</a>, <a href="/wiki/Pakistan" title="Pakistan">Pakistan</a>. He studied at <a href="/wiki/Dayanand_Anglo-Vedic_Schools_System" class="mw-redirect" title="Dayanand Anglo-Vedic Schools System">D.A.V. College</a>, <a href="/wiki/Hailey_College_of_Commerce" title="Hailey College of Commerce">Hailey College of Commerce</a> and <a href="/wiki/Forman_Christian_College_University" class="mw-redirect" title="Forman Christian College University">Forman Christian College</a>, <a href="/wiki/Lahore" title="Lahore">Lahore</a>, all affiliated with the <a href="/wiki/University_of_the_Punjab" title="University of the Punjab">University of the Punjab</a>. He was a member of <a href="/wiki/All_India_Students_Federation" class="mw-redirect" title="All India Students Federation">All India Students Federation</a>. He also participated in the <a href="/wiki/Indian_independence_movement" title="Indian independence movement">Indian independence movement</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-pioneer_5-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-pioneer-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> and was jailed in 1942 during the <a href="/wiki/Quit_India_Movement" title="Quit India Movement">Quit India Movement</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-indep_6-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-indep-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> As a student, he became a member of the <a href="/wiki/Communist_Party_of_India" title="Communist Party of India">Communist Party of India</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-dna_7-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-dna-7">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup> He also has two sisters, Uma Nanda and Sunita Judge. On 26 May 1945, Inder Kumar Gujral married <a href="/wiki/Sheila_Gujral" title="Sheila Gujral">Sheila Gujral</a> (24 January 1924 – 11 July 2011)<sup id="cite_ref-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-10">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> and had two sons, <a href="/wiki/Naresh_Gujral" title="Naresh Gujral">Naresh Gujral</a> (born 19 May 1948), who is a Rajya Sabha MP, and Vishal Gujral. IK Gujral's younger brother <a href="/wiki/Satish_Gujral" title="Satish Gujral">Satish Gujral</a> was a world-renowned painter and sculptor.<sup id="cite_ref-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-11">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Education">Education</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Inder_Kumar_Gujral&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Education">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Gujral's hobbies included poetry;<sup id="cite_ref-hindu_12-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-hindu-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> he spoke <a href="/wiki/Urdu" title="Urdu">Urdu</a> and was, after his death, eulogised as a lover of the language by <a href="/wiki/Maulana_Azad_National_Urdu_University" title="Maulana Azad National Urdu University">Maulana Azad National Urdu University</a>, an institution where he held the position of chancellor.<sup id="cite_ref-UrduUni_13-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-UrduUni-13">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> His wife <a href="/wiki/Sheila_Gujral" title="Sheila Gujral">Sheila Gujral</a>, an acclaimed poet, died on 11 July 2011 after an illness. The couple had two sons, Naresh, who is a <a href="/wiki/Shiromani_Akali_Dal" title="Shiromani Akali Dal">Shiromani Akali Dal</a> MP in the <a href="/wiki/Rajya_Sabha" title="Rajya Sabha">Rajya Sabha</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-ie-nomore_14-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ie-nomore-14">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> and Vishal. The couple also have two granddaughters and a grandson.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Early_politics">Early politics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Inder_Kumar_Gujral&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Early politics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Gujral became vice-president of the <a href="/wiki/New_Delhi_Municipal_Committee" class="mw-redirect" title="New Delhi Municipal Committee">New Delhi Municipal Committee</a> in 1958,<sup id="cite_ref-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-15">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> and joined the Congress party (<a href="/wiki/Indian_National_Congress" title="Indian National Congress">INC</a>) in 1964.<sup id="cite_ref-et_16-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-et-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup> He was close to <a href="/wiki/Indira_Gandhi" title="Indira Gandhi">Indira Gandhi</a>, and became a member of the <a href="/wiki/Rajya_Sabha" title="Rajya Sabha">Rajya Sabha</a> in April 1964. During the <a href="/wiki/The_Emergency_(India)" title="The Emergency (India)">Emergency</a> of June 1975, Gujral was <a href="/wiki/Minister_of_Information_and_Broadcasting" class="mw-redirect" title="Minister of Information and Broadcasting">Minister of Information and Broadcasting</a>, where he was in charge of the media during a time of <a href="/wiki/Censorship_in_India" title="Censorship in India">censorship in India</a> and was in charge of <a href="/wiki/Doordarshan" title="Doordarshan">Doordarshan</a>. He again was selected to the Rajya Sabha to serve until 1976. He also served as Water Resources Minister. Later, Gujral was appointed <a href="/wiki/Ambassador_of_India_to_the_Soviet_Union" class="mw-redirect" title="Ambassador of India to the Soviet Union">Ambassador of India to the Soviet Union</a><sup id="cite_ref-pioneer_5-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-pioneer-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> by Indira Gandhi and stayed on during the tenures of <a href="/wiki/Morarji_Desai" title="Morarji Desai">Morarji Desai</a> and <a href="/wiki/Charan_Singh" title="Charan Singh">Charan Singh</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-dna_7-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-dna-7">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup> He was rumoured to have been shunted out of the ministry due to conflicts with the prime minister's son, <a href="/wiki/Sanjay_Gandhi" title="Sanjay Gandhi">Sanjay Gandhi</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-hindu_12-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-hindu-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-zeenews_17-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-zeenews-17">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup> over media censorship,<sup id="cite_ref-et_16-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-et-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup> and was replaced by <a href="/wiki/Vidya_Charan_Shukla" title="Vidya Charan Shukla">Vidya Charan Shukla</a>, who had no qualms following party lines on the matter; he was then moved to the Planning Ministry.<sup id="cite_ref-dna_7-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-dna-7">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Janata_Dal">Janata Dal</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Inder_Kumar_Gujral&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Janata Dal">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Gujral resigned from the <a href="/wiki/Indian_National_Congress" title="Indian National Congress">Indian National Congress</a> party in the 1980s. Then he joined the <a href="/wiki/Janata_Dal" title="Janata Dal">Janata Dal</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-hindu_12-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-hindu-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-punjabnews_18-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-punjabnews-18">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> In the <a href="/wiki/1989_Indian_general_election" title="1989 Indian general election">1989 Indian general election</a>, Gujral was elected from <a href="/wiki/Jalandhar_(Lok_Sabha_constituency)" class="mw-redirect" title="Jalandhar (Lok Sabha constituency)">Jalandhar</a> in <a href="/wiki/Punjab_(India)" class="mw-redirect" title="Punjab (India)">Punjab</a>. He served as Minister of External Affairs in Prime Minister <a href="/wiki/V._P._Singh" title="V. P. Singh">V. P. Singh</a>'s <a href="/wiki/11th_cabinet_of_India_1989" class="mw-redirect" title="11th cabinet of India 1989">eleventh cabinet of India</a>. In 1989, Singh sent him to <a href="/wiki/Srinagar" title="Srinagar">Srinagar</a> to negotiate with the perpetrators of the <a href="/wiki/1989_kidnapping_of_Rubaiya_Sayeed" class="mw-redirect" title="1989 kidnapping of Rubaiya Sayeed">1989 kidnapping of Rubaiya Sayeed</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-pioneer_5-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-pioneer-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> He also brought about controversy during the <a href="/wiki/Gulf_War" title="Gulf War">Gulf War</a> when he hugged <a href="/wiki/Saddam_Hussein" title="Saddam Hussein">Saddam Hussein</a> as a show-of-good-faith to ensure Indian <a href="/wiki/Expatriate" title="Expatriate">expatriates</a> would be safe.<sup id="cite_ref-et_16-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-et-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup> In the <a href="/wiki/1991_Indian_general_election" title="1991 Indian general election">1991 Indian general election</a>, Gujral contested from <a href="/wiki/Patna_(Lok_Sabha_constituency)" class="mw-redirect" title="Patna (Lok Sabha constituency)">Patna</a> in <a href="/wiki/Bihar" title="Bihar">Bihar</a>. However, the election was countermanded<sup id="cite_ref-pioneer_5-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-pioneer-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> following complaints of 'irregularities'.<sup id="cite_ref-19" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-19">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup> In 1992, Gujral was selected to the <a href="/wiki/Rajya_Sabha" title="Rajya Sabha">Rajya Sabha</a> with the help of <a href="/wiki/Lalu_Prasad_Yadav" title="Lalu Prasad Yadav">Lalu Prasad Yadav</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-pioneer_5-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-pioneer-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Subsequent to the <a href="/wiki/1996_Indian_general_election" title="1996 Indian general election">1996 election</a>, when the <a href="/wiki/United_Front_(India)" title="United Front (India)">United Front</a> government was formed under the premiership of <a href="/wiki/H._D._Deve_Gowda" title="H. D. Deve Gowda">H. D. Deve Gowda</a>, Gujral was again named Minister of External Affairs.<sup id="cite_ref-hindu_12-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-hindu-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-punjabnews_18-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-punjabnews-18">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> During this tenure, he developed the '<a href="/wiki/Gujral_Doctrine" class="mw-redirect" title="Gujral Doctrine">Gujral Doctrine</a>' which emphasised better relations with India's neighbours and was refined when he became prime minister.<sup id="cite_ref-dna_7-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-dna-7">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup> He also served as Union Minister or Minister of State of several other portfolios—Communications and Parliamentary Affairs, Information and Broadcasting, Works and Housing and Planning.<sup id="cite_ref-dailybhaskar_20-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-dailybhaskar-20">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Indian_National_Congress" title="Indian National Congress">Indian National Congress</a> party had been supporting the United Front government from outside, but citing its wish to have the incumbent prime minister replaced, decided to withdraw support;<sup id="cite_ref-et_16-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-et-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup> this led to the government's collapse in April 1997. To avoid mid-term elections, a compromise was reached: the INC agreed to support another United Front government under a new leader, provided its concerns—such as not being consulted before taking important decisions and being marginalised—were addressed. The United Front elected Gujral as its new leader, and he was sworn in as Prime Minister on 21 April 1997.
</p>
<h2><span id="Prime_Minister_.281997.E2.80.931998.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Prime_Minister_(1997–1998)">Prime Minister (1997–1998)</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Inder_Kumar_Gujral&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Prime Minister (1997–1998)">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Gujral became prime minister as the consensus candidate between others that included <a href="/wiki/Lalu_Prasad_Yadav" title="Lalu Prasad Yadav">Lalu Prasad Yadav</a>, <a href="/wiki/Mulayam_Singh_Yadav" title="Mulayam Singh Yadav">Mulayam Singh Yadav</a>;<sup id="cite_ref-dp_21-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-dp-21">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> his government was supported by the INC from outside. In the early weeks of his tenure, the <a href="/wiki/Central_Bureau_of_Investigation" title="Central Bureau of Investigation">Central Bureau of Investigation</a> asked for permission from the <a href="/wiki/Governor_of_Bihar" class="mw-redirect" title="Governor of Bihar">Governor of Bihar</a>, <a href="/wiki/A._R._Kidwai" class="mw-redirect" title="A. R. Kidwai">A. R. Kidwai</a>, to prosecute the state Chief Minister Lalu Prasad Yadav in a corruption case related to the <a href="/wiki/Fodder_Scam" title="Fodder Scam">Fodder Scam</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-22" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-22">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> a move that Kidwai sanctioned. Even legal scholars said that Yadav could not escape prosecution.<sup id="cite_ref-23" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-23">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup> Subsequently, the demand for the resignation of Yadav was raised both from within and outside the United Front. United Front and <a href="/wiki/Telugu_Desam_Party" title="Telugu Desam Party">Telugu Desam Party</a> leader <a href="/wiki/Chandrababu_Naidu" class="mw-redirect" title="Chandrababu Naidu">Chandrababu Naidu</a> and <a href="/wiki/Communist_Party_of_India_(Marxist)" title="Communist Party of India (Marxist)">Communist Party of India (Marxist)</a> General Secretary <a href="/wiki/Harkishen_Singh_Surjeet" class="mw-redirect" title="Harkishen Singh Surjeet">Harkishen Singh Surjeet</a> called for action against Yadav and for the resignation of other RJD members;<sup id="cite_ref-24" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-24">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup> while the same was said by JD members <a href="/wiki/Sharad_Yadav" title="Sharad Yadav">Sharad Yadav</a>, H. D. Deve Gowda and <a href="/wiki/Ram_Vilas_Paswan" title="Ram Vilas Paswan">Ram Vilas Paswan</a> who called for the dismissal of accused RJD members <a href="/wiki/Kanti_Singh" title="Kanti Singh">Kanti Singh</a>, <a href="/wiki/Raghuvansh_Prasad_Singh" title="Raghuvansh Prasad Singh">Raghuvansh Prasad Singh</a> and Captain Jai Narain Nishad. Though INC chairperson <a href="/wiki/Sitaram_Kesri" title="Sitaram Kesri">Sitaram Kesri</a> offered minor calls for Yadav's resignation, Yadav then offered Gujral support to run from any Lok Sabha constituency in Bihar to get his support. Gujral, however, was silent on the matter,<sup id="cite_ref-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-25">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup> but later controversially transferred the CBI director Joginder Singh, who was investigating the case against Yadav, and replaced him with R. C. Sharma, who said Gujral would directly control the CBI and that the pace of investigating many sensational cases "will definitely slacken now."<sup id="cite_ref-26" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-26">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup> However, Yadav was still expelled from the party by JD leader Sharad Yadav, before forming his own <a href="/wiki/Rashtriya_Janata_Dal" title="Rashtriya Janata Dal">Rashtriya Janata Dal</a> in 1997.<sup id="cite_ref-27" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-27">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:President_of_India_Shankar_Dayal_Sharma_administering_the_oath_of_office_of_Prime_Minister_of_India_to_Shri_I._K._Gujral_at_the_Rashtrapati_Bhavan.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/21/President_of_India_Shankar_Dayal_Sharma_administering_the_oath_of_office_of_Prime_Minister_of_India_to_Shri_I._K._Gujral_at_the_Rashtrapati_Bhavan.jpg/220px-President_of_India_Shankar_Dayal_Sharma_administering_the_oath_of_office_of_Prime_Minister_of_India_to_Shri_I._K._Gujral_at_the_Rashtrapati_Bhavan.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="144" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/21/President_of_India_Shankar_Dayal_Sharma_administering_the_oath_of_office_of_Prime_Minister_of_India_to_Shri_I._K._Gujral_at_the_Rashtrapati_Bhavan.jpg/330px-President_of_India_Shankar_Dayal_Sharma_administering_the_oath_of_office_of_Prime_Minister_of_India_to_Shri_I._K._Gujral_at_the_Rashtrapati_Bhavan.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/21/President_of_India_Shankar_Dayal_Sharma_administering_the_oath_of_office_of_Prime_Minister_of_India_to_Shri_I._K._Gujral_at_the_Rashtrapati_Bhavan.jpg/440px-President_of_India_Shankar_Dayal_Sharma_administering_the_oath_of_office_of_Prime_Minister_of_India_to_Shri_I._K._Gujral_at_the_Rashtrapati_Bhavan.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1249" data-file-height="820" /></a><figcaption>President of India <a href="/wiki/Shankar_Dayal_Sharma" title="Shankar Dayal Sharma">Shankar Dayal Sharma</a> administering the oath of office of Prime Minister of India to Shri I. K. Gujral at the <a href="/wiki/Rashtrapati_Bhavan" title="Rashtrapati Bhavan">Rashtrapati Bhavan</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>Another controversial decision of his government was its recommendation of <a href="/wiki/President%27s_rule" title="President&#39;s rule">President's rule</a> in <a href="/wiki/Uttar_Pradesh" title="Uttar Pradesh">Uttar Pradesh</a> in 1997. The <a href="/wiki/Bharatiya_Janata_Party" title="Bharatiya Janata Party">Bharatiya Janata Party</a> (BJP) Uttar Pradesh government, headed by <a href="/wiki/Kalyan_Singh" title="Kalyan Singh">Kalyan Singh</a>, sought a vote of confidence after violence and unruly scenes took place in the assembly. However, President <a href="/wiki/K.R._Narayanan" class="mw-redirect" title="K.R. Narayanan">K.R. Narayanan</a> refused to sign the recommendation and sent it back to the government for reconsideration.<sup id="cite_ref-pioneer_5-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-pioneer-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Allahabad_High_Court" title="Allahabad High Court">Allahabad High Court</a> also gave a decision against President's rule in Uttar Pradesh.<sup id="cite_ref-28" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-28">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup> He also resisted signing the <a href="/wiki/Comprehensive_Test_Ban_Treaty" class="mw-redirect" title="Comprehensive Test Ban Treaty">Comprehensive Test Ban Treaty</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-et_16-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-et-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 28 August 1997, the <a href="/wiki/Jain_Commission" class="mw-redirect" title="Jain Commission">Jain Commission</a> report was submitted to the government<sup id="cite_ref-29" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-29">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> and was leaked on 16 November.<sup id="cite_ref-Jai1999_30-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Jai1999-30">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup> The commission had inquired into the conspiracy aspects of the <a href="/wiki/Rajiv_Gandhi" title="Rajiv Gandhi">Rajiv Gandhi</a> assassination and reportedly criticised the <a href="/wiki/Dravida_Munnetra_Kazhagam" title="Dravida Munnetra Kazhagam">Dravida Munnetra Kazhagam</a> (DMK),<sup id="cite_ref-31" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-31">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup> amongst others such as the <a href="/wiki/P._V._Narasimha_Rao" title="P. V. Narasimha Rao">Narasimha Rao</a> government, for tacitly supporting Tamil militants accused in Gandhi's assassination. The DMK was part of the ruling coalition at the center and had ministers in the Union Cabinet. The Congress first demanded the tabling of the report on the floor of the parliament, which was refused by Gujral, who feared a battle between the DMK and the <a href="/wiki/Tamil_Maanila_Congress" title="Tamil Maanila Congress">Tamil Maanila Congress</a> would lead to the DMK's withdrawal from the government.<sup id="cite_ref-32" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-32">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup> Gujral later formed a Joint Parliamentary Committee to study the report after informing Sitaram Keshri of the decision, to which Keshri acceded. INC parliamentary party leader <a href="/wiki/Sharad_Pawar" title="Sharad Pawar">Sharad Pawar</a> said they would call for the resignation of anyone implicated in the report. Gujral convened the government to inform them of the updates and said it supported the DMK. The DMK's Industry Minister <a href="/wiki/Murasoli_Maran" title="Murasoli Maran">Murasoli Maran</a> said: "We are part of the United Front. We will stand and fall together. I am hundred per cent confident of that. If it were so easy to break the United Front, then it will be called the disunited front. No one is going to ditch their colleagues for a few loaves of power. We have no reason to quit at all. The report is full of recycled news. There is nothing startling about it, everybody already knows what the report is saying. A Madras court is expected to give its verdict on a criminal case on the assassination on January 28. Let us wait till then to know who was involved in the dastardly act. Until then, all this is disinformation." However, the Tamil Maanila Congress called for the DMK, which was in a coalition government in <a href="/wiki/Tamil_Nadu" title="Tamil Nadu">Tamil Nadu</a>, to share all actions it would undertake.<sup id="cite_ref-33" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-33">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup> The report was tabled on 20 November 1997.<sup id="cite_ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup> On the same day there were angry scenes in parliament, as the INC then called for the DMK's removal from the cabinet and refused to partake in any parliamentary debate until that happened. Speaker <a href="/wiki/P.A._Sangma" class="mw-redirect" title="P.A. Sangma">P.A. Sangma</a> then adjourned the house.<sup id="cite_ref-35" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-35">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup> The INC finally withdrew support from his government on 28 November<sup id="cite_ref-nytquit_36-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-nytquit-36">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup> after Gujral sent Kesri a letter saying he would not dismiss any DMK leaders. Gujral resigned following the withdrawal, and sent a letter to President <a href="/wiki/K._R._Narayanan" title="K. R. Narayanan">K. R. Narayanan</a> that read: "My government has lost its majority and does not want to continue in office on moral grounds", but did not call for the dissolution of parliament. The president accepted the resignation, but asked for Gujral to stay on in an interim capacity. INC General Secretary <a href="/wiki/Oscar_Fernandes" title="Oscar Fernandes">Oscar Fernandes</a> then said: "All the secular parties are welcome to support a government which will be attempted by the Congress." The United Front's leader Chandrababu Naidu got the support of all the constituents saying they would neither support the INC nor the <a href="/wiki/Bharatiya_Janata_Party" title="Bharatiya Janata Party">Bharatiya Janata Party</a>, as did the TMC, saying they would not allow a "U.P.-like situation to happen in the centre." In similar measure, BJP leader <a href="/wiki/M._Venkaiah_Naidu" class="mw-redirect" title="M. Venkaiah Naidu">M. Venkaiah Naidu</a> said the party would "throttle" INC attempts to form a new government.<sup id="cite_ref-37" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-37">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup> The president then dissolved parliament on 4 December,<sup id="cite_ref-38" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-38">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup> triggering a <a href="/wiki/Snap_election" title="Snap election">snap election</a>.
</p><p>Gujral was the third PM to be from the <a href="/wiki/Rajya_Sabha" title="Rajya Sabha">Rajya Sabha</a>, following <a href="/wiki/Indira_Gandhi" title="Indira Gandhi">Indira Gandhi</a> (January 1966 - March 1969)<sup id="cite_ref-39" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-39">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup> and <a href="/wiki/H._D._Deve_Gowda" title="H. D. Deve Gowda">H. D. Deve Gowda</a> (June 1996 - April 1997). They were followed by <a href="/wiki/Manmohan_Singh" title="Manmohan Singh">Manmohan Singh</a> (May 2004 - May 2014).<sup id="cite_ref-40" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-40">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>As a Prime minister, Gujral had a practice of reserving Friday mornings for meeting the general public.<sup id="cite_ref-41" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-41">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:The_Minister_of_Information_%26_Broadcasting_and_Culture,_Shri_S._Jaipal_Reddy_releasing_Volume_VI_of_the_Collected_Works_of_Lala_Lajpat_Rai_in_New_Delhi_on_April_20,_2005.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c0/The_Minister_of_Information_%26_Broadcasting_and_Culture%2C_Shri_S._Jaipal_Reddy_releasing_Volume_VI_of_the_Collected_Works_of_Lala_Lajpat_Rai_in_New_Delhi_on_April_20%2C_2005.jpg/220px-thumbnail.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c0/The_Minister_of_Information_%26_Broadcasting_and_Culture%2C_Shri_S._Jaipal_Reddy_releasing_Volume_VI_of_the_Collected_Works_of_Lala_Lajpat_Rai_in_New_Delhi_on_April_20%2C_2005.jpg/330px-thumbnail.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c0/The_Minister_of_Information_%26_Broadcasting_and_Culture%2C_Shri_S._Jaipal_Reddy_releasing_Volume_VI_of_the_Collected_Works_of_Lala_Lajpat_Rai_in_New_Delhi_on_April_20%2C_2005.jpg/440px-thumbnail.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1900" data-file-height="1258" /></a><figcaption>Gujral as <a href="/wiki/Ministry_of_Information_and_Broadcasting_(India)" title="Ministry of Information and Broadcasting (India)">Ministry of Information and Broadcasting</a>.</figcaption></figure>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Gujral_Doctrine">Gujral Doctrine</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Inder_Kumar_Gujral&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Gujral Doctrine">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>The Gujral Doctrine is a set of five principles to guide the conduct of foreign relations with India's <a href="/wiki/SAARC" class="mw-redirect" title="SAARC">immediate neighbours</a>, notably <a href="/wiki/Pakistan" title="Pakistan">Pakistan</a>, as spelt out by Gujral.<sup id="cite_ref-pioneer_5-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-pioneer-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> The doctrine was later termed as such by journalist Bhabani Sen Gupta in his article, <i>India in the Twenty First Century</i> in <a href="/wiki/International_Affairs_(journal)" title="International Affairs (journal)">International Affairs</a>. These principles are, as he set out at <a href="/wiki/Chatham_House" title="Chatham House">Chatham House</a> in September 1996 (which he later reiterated at the <a href="/wiki/Bandaranaike_Centre_for_International_Studies" class="mw-redirect" title="Bandaranaike Centre for International Studies">Bandaranaike Centre for International Studies</a>):<sup id="cite_ref-42" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-42">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1062260506">.mw-parser-output .quotebox{background-color:#F9F9F9;border:1px solid #aaa;box-sizing:border-box;padding:10px;font-size:88%;max-width:100%}.mw-parser-output .quotebox.floatleft{margin:.5em 1.4em .8em 0}.mw-parser-output .quotebox.floatright{margin:.5em 0 .8em 1.4em}.mw-parser-output .quotebox.centered{overflow:hidden;position:relative;margin:.5em auto .8em auto}.mw-parser-output .quotebox.floatleft span,.mw-parser-output .quotebox.floatright span{font-style:inherit}.mw-parser-output .quotebox>blockquote{margin:0;padding:0;border-left:0;font-family:inherit;font-size:inherit}.mw-parser-output .quotebox-title{background-color:#F9F9F9;text-align:center;font-size:110%;font-weight:bold}.mw-parser-output .quotebox-quote>:first-child{margin-top:0}.mw-parser-output .quotebox-quote:last-child>:last-child{margin-bottom:0}.mw-parser-output .quotebox-quote.quoted:before{font-family:"Times New Roman",serif;font-weight:bold;font-size:large;color:gray;content:" “ ";vertical-align:-45%;line-height:0}.mw-parser-output .quotebox-quote.quoted:after{font-family:"Times New Roman",serif;font-weight:bold;font-size:large;color:gray;content:" ” ";line-height:0}.mw-parser-output .quotebox .left-aligned{text-align:left}.mw-parser-output .quotebox .right-aligned{text-align:right}.mw-parser-output .quotebox .center-aligned{text-align:center}.mw-parser-output .quotebox .quote-title,.mw-parser-output .quotebox .quotebox-quote{display:block}.mw-parser-output .quotebox cite{display:block;font-style:normal}@media screen and (max-width:640px){.mw-parser-output .quotebox{width:100%!important;margin:0 0 .8em!important;float:none!important}}</style><div class="quotebox pullquote floatright" style="width:400px; ;">
<blockquote class="quotebox-quote left-aligned" style="">
<p>The United Front Government's neighbourhood policy now stands on five basic principles: First, with the neighbours like Nepal, Bangladesh, Bhutan, Maldives and Sri Lanka, India does not ask for reciprocity but gives all that it can in good faith and trust. Secondly, no South Asian country will allow its territory to be used against the interest of another country of the region. Thirdly, none will interfere in the internal affairs of another. Fourthly, all South Asian countries must respect each other's territorial integrity and sovereignty. And finally, they will settle all their disputes through peaceful bilateral negotiations. These five principles, scrupulously observed, will, I am sure, recast South Asia's regional relationship, including the tormented relationship between India and Pakistan, in a friendly, cooperative mould.
</p>
</blockquote>
</div>
<p>He wrote in his autobiography of the doctrine: "The logic behind the Gujral Doctrine was that since we had to face two hostile neighbours in the north and the west, we had to be at 'total peace' with all other immediate neighbours in order to contain Pakistan's and China's influence in the region."<sup id="cite_ref-dna_7-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-dna-7">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Following a series of attacks throughout the 2000s, said by the Indian media and government to have originated from and been planned in Pakistan, culminating with the <a href="/wiki/2008_Mumbai_attacks" title="2008 Mumbai attacks">2008 Mumbai attacks</a>, the Gujral Doctrine was criticised by the Indian media. Following the attack, <i><a href="/wiki/India_Today" title="India Today">India Today</a></i> said that targeted, covert strikes against Pakistani organisations such as <a href="/wiki/Lashkar-e-Taiba" title="Lashkar-e-Taiba">Lashkar-e-Taiba</a> were a "capability that I.K. Gujral dismantled as prime minister over a decade ago will take over a year to rebuild."<sup id="cite_ref-indiatoday08_43-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-indiatoday08-43">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup> The major setback of the Gujral Doctrine is said to be the debilitating impact it had on the R&amp;AW's (<a href="/wiki/Research_and_Analysis_Wing" title="Research and Analysis Wing">Research and Analysis Wing</a>) ability to conduct operations in <a href="/wiki/Pakistan" title="Pakistan">Pakistan</a>. On his orders Pakistan's special operations desk of R&amp;AW was shut down leading to major gaps in India's intelligence capabilities. Analysts have time and again blamed this as the foremost factor for India's "intelligence failure" before the <a href="/wiki/Kargil_War" title="Kargil War">Kargil War</a> commenced. It is said that this was because of Gujral's negligence towards such repercussions and his urge to leave an imprint on Indo-Pak relations that he did this.<sup id="cite_ref-44" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-44">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup> However, it was also praised in the media.<sup id="cite_ref-45" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-45">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-thehindu1_46-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-thehindu1-46">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-47" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-47">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The snap <a href="/wiki/1998_Indian_general_election" title="1998 Indian general election">election was held in February–March 1998</a>. Gujral contested again from Jalandhar as Janata Dal candidate with the support of the Shiromani Akali Dal. The Akali Dal, though a part of BJP-led coalition, opted to support Gujral because during his Prime Ministerial tenure, Gujral declared that the central government will share the expenses against the <a href="/wiki/Insurgency_in_Punjab" class="mw-redirect" title="Insurgency in Punjab">insurgency in Punjab</a> during the 1980s and early 1990s, along with the state government of <a href="/wiki/Punjab,_India" title="Punjab, India">Punjab</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-pioneer_5-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-pioneer-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Gujral was a member of the <a href="/wiki/Club_de_Madrid" class="mw-redirect" title="Club de Madrid">Club de Madrid</a> after his tenure as the Prime Minister ended.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Illness_and_death">Illness and death</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Inder_Kumar_Gujral&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Illness and death">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:The_Prime_Minister,_Dr._Manmohan_Singh_paying_floral_tributes_at_the_mortal_remains_of_the_former_Prime_Minister,_Shri_Inder_Kumar_Gujral,_in_New_Delhi_on_December_01,_2012.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b9/The_Prime_Minister%2C_Dr._Manmohan_Singh_paying_floral_tributes_at_the_mortal_remains_of_the_former_Prime_Minister%2C_Shri_Inder_Kumar_Gujral%2C_in_New_Delhi_on_December_01%2C_2012.jpg/220px-thumbnail.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="150" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b9/The_Prime_Minister%2C_Dr._Manmohan_Singh_paying_floral_tributes_at_the_mortal_remains_of_the_former_Prime_Minister%2C_Shri_Inder_Kumar_Gujral%2C_in_New_Delhi_on_December_01%2C_2012.jpg/330px-thumbnail.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b9/The_Prime_Minister%2C_Dr._Manmohan_Singh_paying_floral_tributes_at_the_mortal_remains_of_the_former_Prime_Minister%2C_Shri_Inder_Kumar_Gujral%2C_in_New_Delhi_on_December_01%2C_2012.jpg/440px-thumbnail.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2200" data-file-height="1501" /></a><figcaption>Former <a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_India" title="Prime Minister of India">PM</a> <a href="/wiki/Manmohan_Singh" title="Manmohan Singh">Manmohan Singh</a> paying floral tribute to Gujral.</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:The_Vice_President,_Shri_Mohd._Hamid_Ansari_paying_floral_tributes_at_the_Samadhi_of_former_Prime_Minister,_Shri_Inder_Kumar_Gujral,_on_his_death_anniversary,_in_Delhi_on_November_30,_2013.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/94/The_Vice_President%2C_Shri_Mohd._Hamid_Ansari_paying_floral_tributes_at_the_Samadhi_of_former_Prime_Minister%2C_Shri_Inder_Kumar_Gujral%2C_on_his_death_anniversary%2C_in_Delhi_on_November_30%2C_2013.jpg/220px-thumbnail.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="148" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/94/The_Vice_President%2C_Shri_Mohd._Hamid_Ansari_paying_floral_tributes_at_the_Samadhi_of_former_Prime_Minister%2C_Shri_Inder_Kumar_Gujral%2C_on_his_death_anniversary%2C_in_Delhi_on_November_30%2C_2013.jpg/330px-thumbnail.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/94/The_Vice_President%2C_Shri_Mohd._Hamid_Ansari_paying_floral_tributes_at_the_Samadhi_of_former_Prime_Minister%2C_Shri_Inder_Kumar_Gujral%2C_on_his_death_anniversary%2C_in_Delhi_on_November_30%2C_2013.jpg/440px-thumbnail.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2200" data-file-height="1481" /></a><figcaption>Former <a href="/wiki/Vice_President_of_India" title="Vice President of India">Vice President of India</a> <a href="/wiki/Hamid_Ansari" class="mw-redirect" title="Hamid Ansari">Hamid Ansari</a> giving tribute to Gujral on his first death Anniversary (2013).</figcaption></figure>
<p>Gujral was admitted at <a href="/wiki/Medanta" title="Medanta">Medanta</a> Hospital in Gurgaon, Haryana (part of the <a href="/wiki/National_Capital_Region" class="mw-redirect" title="National Capital Region">National Capital Region</a>), on 19 November 2012, after being diagnosed with a <a href="/wiki/Lung_infection" class="mw-redirect" title="Lung infection">lung infection</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-48" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-48">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup> He had had a serious chest infection a few days before being admitted to the hospital<sup id="cite_ref-49" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-49">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup> following more than a year of <a href="/wiki/Kidney_dialysis" title="Kidney dialysis">dialysis</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-dp_21-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-dp-21">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> His health deteriorated in the hospital and was reported to be "very critical". On 27 November, he fell unconscious and his urine output system stopped working. Gujral died on 30 November 2012, four days before his 93rd birthday.<sup id="cite_ref-deathfirstpost_50-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-deathfirstpost-50">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-51" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-51">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-52" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-52">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup> His body <a href="/wiki/Lying_in_state" title="Lying in state">lay in state</a> at his official residence, 5 Janpath, until noon the next day. The <a href="/wiki/Government_of_India" title="Government of India">Government of India</a> declared a seven-day period of state mourning and cancelled official functions until 6 December. He was given a state funeral at 15:00 on 1 December<sup id="cite_ref-pioneer_5-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-pioneer-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> near <a href="/wiki/Samta_sthal" class="mw-redirect" title="Samta sthal">Samata Sthal</a>. His death was announced to parliament by Home Minister <a href="/wiki/Sushil_Kumar_Shinde" class="mw-redirect" title="Sushil Kumar Shinde">Sushil Kumar Shinde</a>, following which both houses adjourned. On 3 December, condolence references were held for him.<sup id="cite_ref-dp_21-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-dp-21">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Reactions came from President <a href="/wiki/Pranab_Mukherjee" title="Pranab Mukherjee">Pranab Mukherjee</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-pioneer_5-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-pioneer-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> Prime Minister <a href="/wiki/Manmohan_Singh" title="Manmohan Singh">Manmohan Singh</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-53" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-53">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup> Bihar Chief Minister <a href="/wiki/Nitish_Kumar" title="Nitish Kumar">Nitish Kumar</a> and RJD chairman <a href="/wiki/Lalu_Prasad_Yadav" title="Lalu Prasad Yadav">Lalu Prasad Yadav</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-54" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-54">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup> Other immediate reactions came from MPs: Law Minister <a href="/wiki/Ashwani_Kumar_(politician)" title="Ashwani Kumar (politician)">Ashwani Kumar</a>, <a href="/wiki/Veerappa_Moily" title="Veerappa Moily">Veerappa Moily</a>, <a href="/wiki/Ghulam_Nabi_Azad" title="Ghulam Nabi Azad">Ghulam Nabi Azad</a>. Minister for New and Renewable Energy <a href="/wiki/Farooq_Abdullah" title="Farooq Abdullah">Farooq Abdullah</a> offered his condolences and said Gujral was "a politician, a diplomat and a humanist who would be remembered for his many accomplishments in the diplomatic and political arena;" while Minister of State for Chemicals and Fertilisers <a href="/wiki/Srikant_Kumar_Jena" title="Srikant Kumar Jena">Srikant Kumar Jena</a> said Gujral had an "exceptional personality, courage and intellect" and that: "Today we feel extremely saddened on the demise of former Prime Minister Shri Inder Kumar Gujral, who was a man of exceptional courage and intellect."<sup id="cite_ref-55" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-55">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup> The Union Cabinet issued a statement that read: "In his death, India has lost a great patriot, a visionary leader and a freedom fighter." INC chairperson <a href="/wiki/Sonia_Gandhi" title="Sonia Gandhi">Sonia Gandhi</a> wrote to Gujral's son, MP <a href="/wiki/Naresh_Gujral" title="Naresh Gujral">Naresh Gujral</a>: "...the late leader had the ability to win goodwill and friendship across the political spectrum. It is these qualities and the genuine warmth of his personality that made him such a widely admired and respected Prime Minister of India, MP and ambassador."<sup id="cite_ref-thehindu1_46-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-thehindu1-46">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup> Sri Lankan President <a href="/wiki/Mahinda_Rajapaksa" title="Mahinda Rajapaksa">Mahinda Rajapaksa</a> sent a message to Indian Prime Minister Manmohan Singh in which he wrote: "Shri Gujral was ahead of his times in a rapidly globalising world. Sri Lanka will always remember with gratitude, Shri I K Gujral's contribution towards strengthening India-Sri Lanka relations and regional cooperation." Pakistani Prime Minister <a href="/wiki/Raja_Parvez_Ashraf" class="mw-redirect" title="Raja Parvez Ashraf">Raja Parvez Ashraf</a> mentioned Gujral's "admirable role" in boosting <a href="/wiki/India-Pakistan_relations" class="mw-redirect" title="India-Pakistan relations">India-Pakistan relations</a>, while he also said South Asia had lost a noble and distinguished politician. Bangladesh Prime Minister <a href="/wiki/Sheikh_Hasina" title="Sheikh Hasina">Sheikh Hasina</a> sent an unnamed senior leader of her <a href="/wiki/Awami_League" title="Awami League">Awami League</a> party for the funeral.<sup id="cite_ref-56" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-56">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Salman_Khurshid" title="Salman Khurshid">Salman Khurshid</a> and <a href="/wiki/Lal_Krishna_Advani" class="mw-redirect" title="Lal Krishna Advani">Lal Krishna Advani</a> were amongst the dignitaries at his funeral.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Global_policy">Global policy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Inder_Kumar_Gujral&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Global policy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Along with his brother <a href="/wiki/Satish_Gujral" title="Satish Gujral">Satish Gujral</a>, he was one of the signatories of the Agreement to convene a convention for drafting a <a href="/wiki/World_constitution" title="World constitution">world constitution</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-57" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-57">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-58" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-58">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup> As a result, for the first time in human history, a <a href="/wiki/World_Constituent_Assembly" class="mw-redirect" title="World Constituent Assembly">World Constituent Assembly</a> convened to draft and adopt the <a href="/wiki/Constitution_for_the_Federation_of_Earth" class="mw-redirect" title="Constitution for the Federation of Earth">Constitution for the Federation of Earth</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-59" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-59">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Honours">Honours</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Inder_Kumar_Gujral&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Honours">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Inder_Kumar_Gujral_2020_stamp_of_India.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/93/Inder_Kumar_Gujral_2020_stamp_of_India.jpg/220px-Inder_Kumar_Gujral_2020_stamp_of_India.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="164" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/93/Inder_Kumar_Gujral_2020_stamp_of_India.jpg/330px-Inder_Kumar_Gujral_2020_stamp_of_India.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/93/Inder_Kumar_Gujral_2020_stamp_of_India.jpg/440px-Inder_Kumar_Gujral_2020_stamp_of_India.jpg 2x" data-file-width="679" data-file-height="505" /></a><figcaption>Gujral on a 2020 stamp of India</figcaption></figure>
<p>Gujral has received several accolades and honours:
</p>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Autobiography">Autobiography</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Inder_Kumar_Gujral&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Autobiography">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="References">References</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Inder_Kumar_Gujral&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: References">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
